where the conditions hold for every $ x_1,\ldots,x_k \in X$ and $1 \leq j < k$.
With the same choice of the $\lambda/\mu$-rules as in
\Cref{Example associative}, the bilinear map $m_X$ becomes
\[
m_X\left(
(f\ast-,-\ast f),(g\ast-,-\ast g)
\right)
=
\bigl(f\ast(g\ast-),(-\ast f)\ast g\bigr).
\]
This makes $\mathcal{E}(X)$ an associative algebra, although it need not be
$k$-nilpotent. For instance, let $X=\mathbb{F}$ be the one-dimensional
abelian algebra. Then
\[
\mathcal{E}(X)
=
\operatorname{End}(X)\times\operatorname{End}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}
\cong\mathbb{F}^2,
\]
which is not nilpotent. Indeed, every linear endomorphism of $X$ is of
the form
\[
\varphi_\alpha\colon x\mapsto\alpha x
\]
for some $\alpha\in\mathbb{F}$, and
\[
m_X\left(
(\varphi_\alpha,\varphi_\beta),
(\varphi_{\alpha'},\varphi_{\beta'})
\right)
=
(\varphi_\alpha\circ\varphi_{\alpha'},
 \varphi_{\beta'}\circ\varphi_\beta)
=
(\varphi_{\alpha\alpha'},\varphi_{\beta'\beta}).
\]
<!-- end example-21 -->
<!-- example-22: body_md -->
\label{exalt}
Let $\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F})\neq2$ and consider the variety $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alt}}$ of alternative algebras. The multilinearisation process shows that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alt}}$ is
equivalently determined by the identities
\[
(xy)z+(xz)y-x(yz)-x(zy)=0
\]
and
\[
(xy)z+(yx)z-x(yz)-y(xz)=0.
\]
A choice of the $\lambda/\mu$-rules is given by
\[
\lambda_1=\lambda_2=-\lambda_7=-\mu_2=\mu_7=\mu_8=1,
\]
with all the remaining coefficients equal to zero. In this case,
$\mathcal{E}(X)$ consists of the pairs
\[
(f\ast-,-\ast f)\in\operatorname{End}(X)^2
\]
satisfying
\[
f\ast(xy)
=
(x\ast f)y+(f\ast x)y-x(f\ast y),
\]
\[
(xy)\ast f
=
x(f\ast y)+x(y\ast f)-(x\ast f)y,
\]
\[
x(y\ast f)
=
(yx)\ast f+(xy)\ast f-y(x\ast f),
\]
and
\[
(f\ast x)y
=
f\ast(yx)+f\ast(xy)-(f\ast y)x
\]
for all $x,y\in X$. The bilinear map
\[
m_X\left(
(f\ast-,-\ast f),(g\ast-,-\ast g)
\right)
=
(h\ast-,-\ast h)
\]
is given by
\[
h\ast x
=
-(f\ast x)\ast g+f\ast(g\ast x)+f\ast(x\ast g)
\]
and
\[
x\ast h
=
(x\ast f)\ast g+(f\ast x)\ast g-f\ast(x\ast g).
\]
Even when $m_X$ defines a bilinear operation on~$\mathcal{E}(X)$, this multiplication need not be alternative. For example, let $X=\mathbb{F}^2$ be the abelian $2$-dimensional algebra. Then, as vector spaces, $\mathcal{E}(X)=\operatorname{End}(X)^2 \cong \operatorname{M}_2(\mathbb{F})^2$, and $m_X$ is total. Identifying $\operatorname{End}(X)$ with $\operatorname{M}_2(\mathbb{F})$ via the canonical basis of $\mathbb{F}^2$, so that composition corresponds to the product of matrices, set
\[
P=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
Q=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
f=(P,0),\quad g=(0,Q).
\]
Writing $\langle u,v\rangle=m_X(u,v)$, direct calculation gives
\[
\langle f,f\rangle=f,\qquad
\langle f,g\rangle=(Q,-Q),\qquad
\langle f,\langle f,g\rangle\rangle=(0,Q).
\]
Thus
\[
\langle\langle f,f\rangle,g\rangle
=(Q,-Q)\neq(0,Q)
=\langle f,\langle f,g\rangle\rangle,
\]
so the left alternative identity fails.
<!-- end example-22 -->
<!-- remark-26: body_md -->
The construction of $\mathcal{E}(X)$ gives an alternative characterisation of
split extensions in $\mathcal{V}$. A split extension of $B$ by $X$ in $\mathcal{V}$ is
equivalent to a homomorphism of partial algebras
\[
\varphi\colon
\widetilde U(B)\to\mathcal{E}(X) \colon b\mapsto(b\ast-,-\ast b),
\]
such that
\[
\Phi_{k,i}(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_k)=0,
\qquad
i=1,\ldots,n_k,
\]
for all the mixed choices described in \Cref{prop_semidirect}.
<!-- end remark-26 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thmV}\cite{WRAAlg}
Let $\mathcal{V}$ be an action accessible, operadic variety of non-associative
algebras.

\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]
For every object $X$ of $\mathcal{V}$, there exists a natural monomorphism of
functors
\[
\tau\colon
\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)
\rightarrowtail
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}}(\widetilde U(-),\mathcal{E}(X)).
\]
For every object $B$ of $\mathcal{V}$, the component $\tau_B$ sends the isomorphism class of a split extension of $B$ by $X$, with associated derived action $(l,r)$, to the homomorphism of partial algebras
\[
\widetilde U(B)\to\mathcal{E}(X) \colon b\mapsto(b\ast-,-\ast b).
\]

\item[(ii)]
Let $B$ and $X$ be objects of $\mathcal{V}$. A homomorphism of partial algebras
\[
\varphi\colon
\widetilde U(B)\to\mathcal{E}(X) \colon b\mapsto(b\ast-,-\ast b)
\]
belongs to $\operatorname{Im}(\tau_B)$ if and only if
\[
\Phi_{k,i}(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_k)=0
\]
for all the mixed choices described in \Cref{prop_semidirect}.

\item[(iii)]
If $D_X=\mathcal{E}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}(X)$ and the resulting total algebra, with multiplication
\[
\langle f,g\rangle=\mu_X(f\otimes g),
\]
is an object of $\mathcal{V}$, then $(\mathcal{E}(X),\tau)$ is a weak representation of
$\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)$.

\item[(iv)]
Suppose that $\mathcal{V}$ is a variety of commutative (respectively, anti-commutative)
algebras, let $\varepsilon=1$ (respectively, $\varepsilon=-1$), and choose the $\lambda/\mu$-rules in the form
\[
\begin{aligned}
x(yz)&=\alpha(xy)z+\beta(xz)y,\\
(yz)x&=\varepsilon\alpha(xy)z+\varepsilon\beta(xz)y,
\end{aligned}
\]
for some $\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{F}$.

Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$ consist of the linear endomorphisms $f\colon X\to X$ for which
\[
\Phi_{k,i}(x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1},f,x_{j+1},\ldots,x_k)=0
\]
for every defining identity $\Phi_{k,i}$, every $1\leq j\leq k$,
and every choice of the other variables in $X$. Products involving
$f$ are evaluated using $fx=f(x)$ and $xf=\varepsilon f(x)$. Consider the bilinear map
\[
m_X^\varepsilon\colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\times \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\to\operatorname{End}(X) \colon (f,g)
\mapsto \varepsilon\bigl(\alpha(g\circ f)+\beta(f\circ g)\bigr),
\]
and let
\[
\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon\colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\otimes \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\to\operatorname{End}(X)
\]
be its linearisation. Define
\[
D_X^\varepsilon
=\left(\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon\right)^{-1}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X),
\qquad
\mu_X^\varepsilon
=\left.\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon\right|_{D_X^\varepsilon}.
\]
Then the linear map $j_X\colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\to\mathcal{E}(X)$, defined by
$j_X(f)=(f,\varepsilon f)$, is an isomorphism of partial algebras
\[
(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X,D_X^\varepsilon,\mu_X^\varepsilon)
\cong(\mathcal{E}(X),D_X,\mu_X).
\]
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
{\ }
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] Let $(l,r)$ be the derived action associated with a split extension of $B$ by $X$, and set $\varphi(b)=(b\ast-,-\ast b)$. \Cref{prop_semidirect}, applied to the mixed choices containing exactly one element of $B$, gives $\varphi(b)\in\mathcal{E}(X)$. Applying the fixed $\lambda/\mu$-rules in the semidirect product gives
$m_X(\varphi(b),\varphi(b'))=\varphi(bb')\in\mathcal{E}(X)$; hence
$\varphi(b)\otimes\varphi(b')\in D_X$ and
$\mu_X(\varphi(b)\otimes\varphi(b'))=\varphi(bb')$, so $\varphi$ is a
homomorphism of partial algebras by \Cref{lem:morphism-from-total}.
Since $(l,r)$ is recovered from $\varphi$, each $\tau_B$ is injective;
naturality follows from the fact that change of base along
$g\colon B'\to B$ acts on derived actions by precomposition with~$g$.

\item[(ii)] If $\varphi$ satisfies the mixed identities, then
$(B\oplus X,\cdot)$ is an object of $\mathcal{V}$ by \Cref{prop_semidirect},
and $\varphi$ is the image of the corresponding split extension. The
converse is clear.

\item[(iii)] If $D_X=\mathcal{E}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}(X)$, then
$(\mathcal{E}(X),D_X,\mu_X)=\widetilde U(\mathcal{E}(X))$, where $\mathcal{E}(X)$ is regarded as
an algebra with multiplication~$m_X$. Since $\widetilde U$ is fully
faithful, if this algebra lies in $\mathcal{V}$ we get
$\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}}(\widetilde U(-),\mathcal{E}(X))\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{V}}(-,\mathcal{E}(X))$, and
$\tau$ is a weak representation.

\item[(iv)] The mixed commutativity or anti-commutativity identity implies that every element of $\mathcal{E}(X)$ has the form $(f,\varepsilon f)$. The remaining identities with one operator identify $\mathcal{E}(X)$, as a vector space, with $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$ via $j_X$. Using the chosen $\lambda/\mu$-rules, we get
\[
m_X(j_X(f),j_X(g))
=\bigl(m_X^\varepsilon(f,g),
       \varepsilon m_X^\varepsilon(f,g)\bigr).
\]
By linearity, for every $t\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\otimes \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$,
\[
\widetilde m_X\bigl((j_X\otimes j_X)(t)\bigr)
=\bigl(\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon(t),
       \varepsilon\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon(t)\bigr).
\]
This pair belongs to $\mathcal{E}(X)=j_X(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X)$ if and only if
$\widetilde m_X^\varepsilon(t)\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$.
Hence $j_X\otimes j_X$ identifies $D_X^\varepsilon$ with $D_X$,
and $j_X$ preserves the partial multiplications.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- definition-19: body_md -->
\cite{WRAAlg}
The partial algebra $\mathcal{E}(X)$ is called the \emph{external weak actor} of~$X$, and the pair $(\mathcal{E}(X),\tau)$ is called an \emph{external weak representation} of the functor $\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)$. When $\tau$ is a natural isomorphism of functors, $\mathcal{E}(X)$ is called the \emph{external actor} of $X$.
<!-- end definition-19 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\cite{WRAAlg}
Let $\mathbb{F}$ be a field with $\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F})\neq 2,3$, and let $\mathcal{V}$ be a non-abelian, action accessible, operadic, quadratic variety of non-associative $\mathbb{F}$-algebras.

\begin{enumerate}
\item[(1)]
If $\mathcal{V}$ is a variety of commutative algebras, then it coincides
with one of
\[
\ensuremath{\mathsf{CAssoc}},\qquad \ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}},\qquad \ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Com}}).
\]

\item[(2)]
If $\mathcal{V}$ is a variety of anti-commutative algebras, then it coincides
with one of
\[
\ensuremath{\mathsf{Lie}},\qquad \ensuremath{\mathsf{ACAAssoc}},\qquad \ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACom}}).
\]
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- examples-2: body_md -->
\label{ExamplesEX}{\ }
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(1)] If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{AbAlg}}$, we choose
\[
x(yz)=(yz)x=0.
\]
Then $\mathcal{E}(X)=0$ is the actor of $X$.

\item[(2)]
If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{CAssoc}}$, we choose
\[
x(yz)=(xz)y,
\qquad
(yz)x=(xz)y.
\]
With this choice, $\mathcal{E}(X)\cong\operatorname{M}(X)$ is an associative
algebra, with multiplication induced by the usual composition of endomorphisms. Moreover, there is a natural isomorphism
\[
\operatorname{SplExt}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{CAssoc}}}(-,X)
\cong
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{Assoc}}}(\overline{U}(-),\operatorname{M}(X)),
\]
where $\overline{U}\colon\ensuremath{\mathsf{CAssoc}}\to\ensuremath{\mathsf{Assoc}}$ denotes the
forgetful functor and $\operatorname{M}(X)$ is the associative algebra
of multipliers of $X$. Hence $\mathcal{E}(X)\cong\operatorname{M}(X)$ is an external actor of $X$.

\item[(3)]
If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Lie}}$ and $\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F})\neq2$, we choose
\[
\begin{aligned}
x(yz)&=(xy)z-(xz)y,\\
(yz)x&=-(xy)z+(xz)y.
\end{aligned}
\]
Then $\mathcal{E}(X)\cong\operatorname{Der}(X)$, with the usual commutator bracket, is the actor of~$X$.

\item[(4)]
Let $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$ be the variety of Jacobi--Jordan algebras. We choose
\[
\begin{aligned}
x(yz)&=-(xy)z-(xz)y,\\
(yz)x&=-(xy)z-(xz)y.
\end{aligned}
\]
For any Jacobi--Jordan algebra $X$, let
\[
\operatorname{ADer}(X)=
\left\{
d\in\operatorname{End}(X)\ \middle|\
d(xy)=-d(x)y-xd(y),\; \forall x,y\in X
\right\}
\]
be the vector space of \emph{anti-derivations} of $X$.
Consider the linear map
\[
\widetilde m_X\colon\operatorname{ADer}(X)\otimes\operatorname{ADer}(X)\to\operatorname{End}(X)
\]
determined on pure tensors by
\[
\widetilde m_X(d\otimes d')=-d\circ d'-d'\circ d,
\]
and put
\[
D_X=\widetilde m_X^{-1}\bigl(\operatorname{ADer}(X)\bigr),\qquad
\mu_X=\widetilde m_X|_{D_X}.
\]
This defines the partial algebra
\[
P_X=\bigl(\operatorname{ADer}(X),D_X,\mu_X\bigr),
\]
whose partial multiplication is commutative.
For $d,d'\in\operatorname{ADer}(X)$, their product is defined if and only if
$-d\circ d'-d'\circ d$ is an anti-derivation.
Whenever it is defined, the product is given by
\[
\langle d,d'\rangle=-d\circ d'-d'\circ d.
\]

To see that $P_X$ is an external actor, let $B$ be an object of $\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$. A linear map $\varphi\colon B\to\operatorname{ADer}(X)$ defines a homomorphism
$\widetilde U(B)\to P_X$ if and only if
\[
\varphi(bb')
=-\varphi(b)\circ\varphi(b')
 -\varphi(b')\circ\varphi(b)
\]
for every $b,b' \in B$. The condition $\varphi(b)\in\operatorname{ADer}(X)$ expresses the mixed
Jacobi identities with one element of $B$, while the displayed equation expresses those with two elements of $B$. Thus,
\[
l(b,x)=r(x,b)=\varphi(b)(x)
\]
define a derived action of $B$ on $X$.
Conversely, every derived action has this form and determines a
unique such homomorphism. This correspondence is natural in $B$,
giving
\[
\operatorname{SplExt}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}}(-,X)
\cong
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}}\bigl(\widetilde U(-),P_X\bigr).
\]
With the above choice of the $\lambda/\mu$-rules,
\Cref{thmV}(iv), applied with $\varepsilon=1$ and
$\alpha=\beta=-1$, gives an isomorphism
$\mathcal{E}(X)\cong P_X$ of partial algebras; see
\cite[Theorem~2.17]{WRAAlg}.
If $\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F})=2$, then $\operatorname{ADer}(X)=\operatorname{Der}(X)$ and
$\langle d,d'\rangle=[d,d']$.
In this case, the bracket is symmetric
and satisfies the Jacobi identity.
Hence, $P_X$ is an object of $\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$, and the above natural isomorphism shows that $\operatorname{Der}(X)$ is the actor of $X$. Thus, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$ is action representable in
characteristic $2$; this also follows from~\cite[Theorem~1.12]{Tim}, since in characteristic~$2$ the variety $\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$ coincides with the variety of quasi-Lie algebras considered there. When $\operatorname{char}(\mathbb{F})\neq2$, it remains open whether $\ensuremath{\mathsf{JJord}}$ is weakly action representable.

\item[(5)]
Let $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACAAssoc}}$ be the variety of anti-commutative
anti-associative algebras. We choose
\[
x(yz)=(xz)y,
\qquad
(yz)x=-(xz)y.
\]
For any anti-commutative anti-associative algebra $X$, we define the vector space
\[
\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X=
\left\{
f\in\operatorname{End}(X)\ \middle|\
f(xy)=-f(x)y,\; \forall x,y\in X
\right\}.
\]
Consider the linear map
\[
\widetilde m_X\colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\otimes \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X\to\operatorname{End}(X) \colon f\otimes g \mapsto -f\circ g,
\]
and put
\[
D_X=\widetilde m_X^{-1}(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X),\qquad
\mu_X=\widetilde m_X|_{D_X}.
\]
This defines the partial algebra
\[
P_X=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X,D_X,\mu_X).
\]
For $f,g\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$, their product is defined if and only if
$-f\circ g\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$, and then
\[
\langle f,g\rangle=-f\circ g.
\]
The partial multiplication is associative wherever both iterated
products are defined, since
\[
\langle\langle f,g\rangle,h\rangle
=(f\circ g)\circ h=f \circ (g \circ h)
=\langle f,\langle g,h\rangle\rangle.
\]
Let $B$ be an object of $\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACAAssoc}}$. A linear map $\varphi\colon B\to \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$
defines a homomorphism $\widetilde U(B)\to P_X$ if and only if
\[
\varphi(bb')=-\varphi(b)\circ\varphi(b')
\]
for every $b,b' \in B$. The condition $\varphi(b)\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X$ expresses the mixed
anti-associative identities with one element of $B$.
The displayed equation, together with the anti-commutativity
of $B$, expresses those with two elements of $B$.
Thus
\[
l(b,x)=-r(x,b)=\varphi(b)(x)
\]
define a derived action of $B$ on $X$. Conversely, every derived
action determines a unique such homomorphism.
Naturality in $B$ therefore gives
\[
\operatorname{SplExt}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACAAssoc}}}(-,X)
\cong
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}}\bigl(\widetilde U(-),P_X\bigr).
\]
With the above choice of the $\lambda/\mu$-rules,
\Cref{thmV}(iv), applied with $\varepsilon=-1$,
$\alpha=0$ and $\beta=1$, yields an isomorphism
$\mathcal{E}(X)\cong P_X$ of partial algebras; see
\cite[Theorem~2.20]{WRAAlg}.
It remains open whether $\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACAAssoc}}$ is weakly action representable.

\item[(6)]
If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Com}})$ or $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACom}})$, we choose
\[
x(yz)=(yz)x=0.
\]
Then $\mathcal{E}(X)$ is isomorphic to the abelian algebra
\[
\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_X=\left\{
f\in\operatorname{End}(X)
\mid
f(xy)=f(x)y=0,\; \forall x,y\in X
\right\}.
\]
It follows from \Cref{thmV}(iii) that both
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Com}})$ and $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{ACom}})$ are weakly action
representable; see also~\cite[Theorems~2.19 and~2.21]{WRAAlg}.

\item[(7)] If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Assoc}}$ and we choose the $\lambda/\mu$-rules as in \Cref{Example associative}, then $\mathcal{E}(X)\cong\operatorname{Bim}(X)$, the associative algebra of bimultipliers of $X$. Thus, as already stated in \Cref{USGA_Assoc}, the variety $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Assoc}}$ is weakly action representable.

\item[(8)] Let $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Leib}}$ and choose the $\lambda/\mu$-rules as in \Cref{esLeib}. Then $\mathcal{E}(X)\cong\operatorname{Bider}(X)$, the Leibniz algebra of biderivations of $X$. It follows that the variety $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Leib}}$ is weakly action representable; see~\cite{CigoliManciniMetere}.

\item[(9)]
If $\mathcal{V}=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alg}})$, we choose again
\[
x(yz)=(yz)x=0.
\]
Then, as observed in \Cref{exnil2}, $\mathcal{E}(X)$ is an abelian
algebra. Thus, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_2(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alg}})$ is weakly action representable.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end examples-2 -->
<!-- remark-27: body_md -->
By the results recalled at the end of \Cref{SecIntAct}, there are action
accessible varieties for which no choice of the $\lambda/\mu$-rules
makes $\mathcal{E}(X)$ a total algebra in the variety for every object $X$.
Indeed, the varieties $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Nil}}_k(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Lie}})$ of $k$-nilpotent and $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Sol}}_n(\ensuremath{\mathsf{Lie}})$ of $n$-solvable Lie algebras are not weakly action representable for $k\geq3$ and $n\geq2$, respectively~\cite{XabiMancini}.
Over a field of characteristic different from~$2$, they are action accessible, operadic varieties, so that the assertion follows from \Cref{thmV}(iii).
<!-- end remark-27 -->
<!-- remark-28: body_md -->
The construction of the vector space $\mathcal{E}(X)$ can also be carried out for an operadic variety $\mathcal{V}$ which is not action accessible. In that case, however, there is no canonical way to
endow $\mathcal{E}(X)$ with a bilinear map $m_X$ as in
\Cref{E(X)}. We therefore obtain only a natural monomorphism of
functors
\[
\tau\colon
\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)
\rightarrowtail
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{Vec}}}(\hat{U}(-),\mathcal{E}(X)),
\]
where $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Vec}}$ is the category of $\mathbb{F}$-vector spaces and
$\hat{U}\colon\mathcal{V}\to\ensuremath{\mathsf{Vec}}$ is the forgetful functor.
<!-- end remark-28 -->
<!-- remark-29: body_md -->
\Cref{Theorem AC iff Orzech} shows that a homogeneous variety of
non-associative algebras is an Orzech category of interest if and
only if it is action accessible. As observed in \Cref{USGA_var},
a formulation of Axiom~2 is determined by a choice of constants
\[
\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_8,\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_8\in\mathbb{F}
\]
defining the $\lambda/\mu$-rules. Moreover, in this case, $\mathcal{V}'=\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alg}}$, the category of all non-associative algebras.
<!-- end remark-29 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{V}$ be an action accessible, operadic variety of
non-associative algebras, together with a choice of constants
\[
\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_8,\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_8\in\mathbb{F}
\]
defining the $\lambda/\mu$-rules. For every object $X$ of $\mathcal{V}$,
there are natural monomorphisms of functors
\[
\mu\colon
\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)
\rightarrowtail
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alg}}}(U(-),\operatorname{USGA}(X))
\]
and
\[
\tau\colon
\operatorname{SplExt}(-,X)
\rightarrowtail
\operatorname{Hom}_{\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}}(\widetilde U(-),\mathcal{E}(X)),
\]
where
\[
U\colon\mathcal{V}\to\ensuremath{\mathsf{Alg}}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\widetilde U\colon\mathcal{V}\to\ensuremath{\mathsf{PAlg}}
\]
are the forgetful functors.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
